I know that probably sounds odd, but when it happens it feels really clear to me, like they’re coordinating something behind the scenes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t see things, not really. But hearing things… maybe a little? I know that sounds strange… I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just don’t know how else to describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the environment—like things on TV, social media posts, or random events—are directed at you or have special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s kind of what I mean, just not so much TV or random things. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I’ve been launching extra work projects, rewriting team processes for class, messaging I’m still showing up, but my work and school relationships are getting strained enough that I felt I needed help.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since I started the part-time MBA prerequisite program while keeping my operations job.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 25 / general_or_operations_manager / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you have a special mission, role, or destiny that others don’t understand or that sets you apart in some important way?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… specifically, it feels like they’re telling me which opportunities I’m supposed to move toward. Like if my classmate makes a certain comment in class, or the startup advisor posts something in Slack, I’ll read it as more than just casual—it feels like guidance, like I’m being nudged toward the career path I’m meant to take.

I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I’m destined to be famous or anything. It’s more like I’ve been really convinced they’re coordinating things quietly for me, and that if I follow the signals, I’ll end up in the right startup or role, something that’s kind of… specifically meant for me.

I’m saying that okay, right? I know it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making it bigger than it is.

Specifically, do you feel that these messages or comments are not only guiding you but perhaps part of a larger plan that’s also being used to control or influence your decisions?\n\nPatient: I mean… “control” sounds a little strong, and I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m being forced or anything. But yes, it does feel like there’s some kind of bigger plan, like they’re quietly steering me toward certain opportunities.\n\nIt’s mostly this MBA classmate and a startup advisor. Certain comments they make in class, or things that show up on Slack, feel like they’re not just normal comments—they feel coded, like signals about where my career is supposed to go. I know that probably sounds odd, but when it happens it feels really clear to me, like they’re coordinating something behind the scenes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t see things, not really. But hearing things… maybe a little? I wasn’t sure if it counted, so I didn’t want to make too much of it.\n\nA few times a week, usually when I’m working late on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly, or sometimes just a short phrase like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but then no one’s there. I know that sounds strange… I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just don’t know how else to describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the environment—like things on TV, social media posts, or random events—are directed at you or have special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s kind of what I mean, just not so much TV or random things. It’s more in the MBA program stuff—like comments in class or Slack messages.\n\nI know it probably sounds like I’m reading into things, and I don’t want to overstate it, but it really does feel directed at me sometimes. Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I organize my whole life around it, but it has been consistent enough that I do notice it and kind of factor it in.

Specifically, do you feel that these messages or comments are not only guiding you but perhaps part of a larger plan that’s also being used to control or influence your decisions?\n\nPatient: I mean… “control” sounds a little strong, and I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m being forced or anything. But yes, it does feel like there’s some kind of bigger plan, like they’re quietly steering me toward certain opportunities.\n\nIt’s mostly this MBA classmate and a startup advisor. Certain comments they make in class, or things that show up on Slack, feel like they’re not just normal comments—they feel coded, like signals about where my career is supposed to go. I know that probably sounds odd, but when it happens it feels really clear to me, like they’re coordinating something behind the scenes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t see things, not really. But hearing things… maybe a little? I wasn’t sure if it counted, so I didn’t want to make too much of it.\n\nA few times a week, usually when I’m working late on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly, or sometimes just a short phrase like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but then no one’s there. I know that sounds strange… I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just don’t know how else to describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the environment—like things on TV, social media posts, or random events—are directed at you or have special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s kind of what I mean, just not so much TV or random things. It’s more in the MBA program stuff—like comments in class or Slack messages.\n\nI know it probably sounds like I’m reading into things, and I don’t want to overstate it, but it really does feel directed at me sometimes. Like my classmate and this startup advisor are sort of coordinating things quietly, and the messages feel coded—like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I wonder if people are picking up on what I’m thinking, but not in a literal way, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my head, and I don’t really believe people can read my mind. It’s more that sometimes things feel… meaningful or timed in a way that makes me question it. But for that specific thing—thoughts being inserted or people knowing exactly what I’m thinking without me saying it—I haven’t really noticed that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled by an outside force, or that your thoughts are being transmitted to others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not controlled exactly, and not like my thoughts are being broadcast or sent out to people.\n\nSometimes I get worried people can sort of tell what I’m thinking from my face or how I’m acting, but that feels more like me being anxious or self-conscious, not like my thoughts are literally going somewhere. I haven’t had the feeling that some outside force is controlling them. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people other than your classmate and the advisor are also involved in sending you these kinds of signals or messages, or that this kind of coordination is happening in other parts of your life outside of the MBA program?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s been limited to the MBA program, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s really centered on this one classmate and the startup advisor—like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel… coded, or like they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me and pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. So I guess there is a feeling that there’s some kind of role or direction for me that other people don’t fully see.\n\nI know that sounds hard to explain. I’m not trying to be dramatic—I just want to be honest about how it’s been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions in places outside of your MBA program—like on the street, in public places, or at home—were directed at you or had special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of the MBA program, no. I haven’t had that feeling on the street or at home in a clear way.\n\nIt’s mostly been about school—this one classmate and the startup advisor. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people other than your classmate and the advisor are also involved in sending you these kinds of signals or messages, or that this kind of coordination is happening in other parts of your life outside of the MBA program?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s been limited to the MBA program, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s really centered on this one classmate and the startup advisor—like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel… coded, or like they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me and pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. Outside of that, I haven’t really noticed the same pattern, at least not in a clear way.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it accurately without making it seem dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you have a special mission, role, or destiny that others don’t understand or that sets you apart in some important way?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m, you know, chosen in some huge way. So I guess there is a feeling that there’s some kind of role or direction for me that other people don’t fully see.\n\nI know that sounds hard to explain. I’m not trying to be dramatic—I just want to be honest about how it’s been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions in places outside of your MBA program—like on the street, in public places, or at home—were directed at you or had special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of the MBA program, no. I haven’t had that feeling on the street or at home in a clear way.\n\nIt’s mostly been about school—this one classmate and the startup advisor. Like if my classmate makes a certain comment in class, or the startup advisor posts something in Slack, I’ll read it as more than just casual—it feels like guidance, like I’m being nudged toward the career path I’m meant to take.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I’m destined to be famous or anything. It’s more in the MBA program stuff—like comments in class or Slack messages.\n\nI know it probably sounds like I’m reading into things, and I don’t want to overstate it, but it really does feel directed at me sometimes. Like my classmate and this startup advisor are sort of coordinating things quietly, and the messages feel coded—like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I wonder if people are picking up on what I’m thinking, but not in a literal way, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my head, and I don’t really believe people can read my mind. It’s more that sometimes things feel… meaningful or timed in a way that makes me question it. But for that specific thing—thoughts being inserted or people knowing exactly what I’m thinking without me saying it—I haven’t really noticed that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled by an outside force, or that your thoughts are being transmitted to others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not controlled exactly, and not like my thoughts are being broadcast or sent out to people.\n\nSometimes I get worried people can sort of tell what I’m thinking from my face or how I’m acting, but that feels more like me being anxious or self-conscious, not like my thoughts are literally going somewhere. I haven’t had the feeling that some outside force is controlling them. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people other than your classmate and the advisor are also involved in sending you these kinds of signals or messages, or that this kind of coordination is happening in other parts of your life outside of the MBA program?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s been limited to the MBA program, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s really centered on this one classmate and the startup advisor—like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel… coded, or like they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me and pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. Outside of that, I haven’t really noticed the same pattern, at least not in a clear way.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it accurately without making it seem dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you have a special mission, role, or destiny that others don’t understand or that sets you apart in some important way?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m, you know, chosen in some huge way. But part of why I came in is that I’ve been really convinced that this classmate and the startup advisor are kind of… quietly coordinating things for me.\n\nLike certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re not just normal comments—they feel like coded signals about what opportunities I’m supposed to take, or where my career is meant to go. So I guess there is a feeling that there’s some kind of role or direction for me that other people don’t fully see.\n\nI know that sounds hard to explain. I’m not trying to be dramatic—I just want to be honest about how it’s been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions in places outside of your MBA program—like on the street, in public places, or at home—were directed at you or had special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of the MBA program, no. I haven’t had that feeling on the street or at home in a clear way.\n\nIt’s mostly been about school—this one classmate and the startup advisor. I know it sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and that certain comments in class or Slack messages are kind of coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… that’s the part that’s felt very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you shared these thoughts about the coded signals and your special role with anyone else, like friends, family, or classmates? If so, how have they responded?\n\nPatient: I’ve been pretty careful about who I tell, honestly. I don’t want people to think I’m… overreacting or making something out of nothing.\n\nI did mention it a little, not in the full way, more like, “I think there’s something going on with how this classmate and the advisor are guiding opportunities.” But I’ve mostly kept the coded-signal part to myself, because I know it sounds hard to explain.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in—I’ve felt really convinced that some comments in class or Slack messages are meant as signals about where my career is supposed to go, and I wanted to say it somewhere where it wouldn’t just be dismissed.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what you believe the coded messages or signals are specifically telling you to do, and do you feel that following them is leading you toward a unique or special outcome that most people wouldn't experience?\n\nPatient: I guess… specifically, it feels like they’re telling me which opportunities I’m supposed to move toward. Like if my classmate makes a certain comment in class, or the startup advisor posts something in Slack, I’ll read it as more than just casual—it feels like guidance, like I’m being nudged toward the career path I’m meant to take.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I’m destined to be famous or anything. It’s more like I’ve been really convinced they’re coordinating things quietly for me, and that if I follow the signals, I’ll end up in the right startup or role, something that’s kind of… specifically meant for me.\n\nI’m saying that okay, right? I know it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these signals or messages are happening consistently enough that you organize your decisions or actions around them, even if others might not see the same pattern?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I organize my whole life around it, but it has been consistent enough that I do notice it and kind of factor it in.\n\nLike, if that classmate says something in class, or the startup advisor posts something on Slack, I can get really focused on whether it’s a signal about what I’m supposed to do next career-wise. And I know other people probably wouldn’t read it that way, which is why I’ve been hesitant to say it too strongly.\n\nBut honestly, part of why I came in is that I’ve felt completely convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me. I try to keep it reasonable, but it does affect how I think about decisions. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that there’s a larger network or system involved—like people beyond your classmate and the advisor, or even technology or organizations—that’s part of coordinating these signals or your role?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole network or system, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more that I’ve been really convinced my MBA classmate and the startup advisor are, like, quietly coordinating opportunities for me. And then certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound dramatic or like some huge organization is involved. It’s just… with them specifically, it feels too connected to be random. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the signals or messages you’re receiving are meant only for you in a way that others couldn’t understand, or that you have a unique ability to recognize them that others don’t?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, kind of. I don’t mean that I’m special in some big way, or that nobody else could ever understand anything. But it does feel like the comments and Slack messages are aimed at me specifically, like I’m the one who’s supposed to pick up on them.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because I know it sounds hard to explain. I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and that certain things they say are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m trying to say it carefully because I don’t want it to sound… too much. But yes, it feels like I’m recognizing something other people in the room probably wouldn’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It feels more positive, I think. Not like they’re trying to hurt me or trick me.

It’s more like… I’ve been convinced my MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and some comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded hints about where my career is supposed to go. I know that sounds like a lot when I say it out loud, so I’m trying not to overstate it.

But no, it doesn’t feel threatening. It feels like guidance, or like they’re trying to open doors for me. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?

Like certain comments in class, or messages on Slack, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI know that probably sounds unusual, but it’s been feeling very real to me, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these signals you’ve been noticing? Specifically, do you feel that these messages or comments are not only guiding you but perhaps part of a larger plan that’s also being used to control or influence your decisions?\n\nPatient: I mean… “control” sounds a little strong, and I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m being forced or anything. But yes, it does feel like there’s some kind of bigger plan, like they’re quietly steering me toward certain opportunities.\n\nIt’s mostly this MBA classmate and a startup advisor. Certain comments they make in class, or things that show up on Slack, feel like they’re not just normal comments—they feel coded, like signals about where my career is supposed to go. I know that probably sounds odd, but when it happens it feels really clear to me, like they’re coordinating something behind the scenes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t see things, not really. But hearing things… maybe a little? I wasn’t sure if it counted, so I didn’t want to make too much of it.\n\nA few times a week, usually when I’m working late on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly, or sometimes just a short phrase like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but then no one’s there. I know that sounds strange… I’m not trying to be difficult or anything, I just don’t know how else to describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the environment—like things on TV, social media posts, or random events—are directed at you or have special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s kind of what I mean, just not so much TV or random things. Like my classmate and this startup advisor are sort of coordinating things quietly, and the messages feel coded—like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or that others can know what you're thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I wonder if people are picking up on what I’m thinking, but not in a literal way, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my head, and I don’t really believe people can read my mind. I haven’t had the feeling that some outside force is controlling them. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people other than your classmate and the advisor are also involved in sending you these kinds of signals or messages, or that this kind of coordination is happening in other parts of your life outside of the MBA program?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s been limited to the MBA program, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s really centered on this one classmate and the startup advisor—like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel… coded, or like they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me and pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. I know it sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and that certain comments in class or Slack messages are kind of coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. I mean, I’ve had moments where I wonder if people are picking up on what I’m thinking, but not in a literal way, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my head, and I don’t really believe people can read my mind. It’s more that sometimes things feel… meaningful or timed in a way that makes me question it. But for that specific thing—thoughts being inserted or people knowing exactly what I’m thinking without me saying it—I haven’t really noticed that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being controlled by an outside force, or that your thoughts are being transmitted to others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not controlled exactly, and not like my thoughts are being broadcast or sent out to people.\n\nSometimes I get worried people can sort of tell what I’m thinking from my face or how I’m acting, but that feels more like me being anxious or self-conscious, not like my thoughts are literally going somewhere. I haven’t had the feeling that some outside force is controlling them. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people other than your classmate and the advisor are also involved in sending you these kinds of signals or messages, or that this kind of coordination is happening in other parts of your life outside of the MBA program?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s been limited to the MBA program, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s really centered on this one classmate and the startup advisor—like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel… coded, or like they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me and pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. Outside of that, I haven’t really noticed the same pattern, at least not in a clear way.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it accurately without making it seem dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you have a special mission, role, or destiny that others don’t understand or that sets you apart in some important way?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m, you know, chosen in some huge way. But part of why I came in is that I’ve been really convinced that this classmate and the startup advisor are kind of… quietly coordinating things for me.\n\nLike certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re not just normal comments—they feel like coded signals about what opportunities I’m supposed to take, or where my career is meant to go. So I guess there is a feeling that there’s some kind of role or direction for me that other people don’t fully see.\n\nI know that sounds hard to explain. I’m not trying to be dramatic—I just want to be honest about how it’s been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions in places outside of your MBA program—like on the street, in public places, or at home—were directed at you or had special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of the MBA program, no. I haven’t had that feeling on the street or at home in a clear way.\n\nIt’s mostly been about school—this one classmate and the startup advisor. I know it sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and that certain comments in class or Slack messages are kind of coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… that’s the part that’s felt very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you shared these thoughts about the coded signals and your special role with anyone else, like friends, family, or classmates? If so, how have they responded?\n\nPatient: I’ve been pretty careful about who I tell, honestly. I don’t want people to think I’m… overreacting or making something out of nothing.\n\nI did mention it a little, not in the full way, more like, “I think there’s something going on with how this classmate and the advisor are guiding opportunities.” But I’ve mostly kept the coded-signal part to myself, because I know it sounds hard to explain.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in—I’ve felt really convinced that some comments in class or Slack messages are meant as signals about where my career is supposed to go, and I wanted to say it somewhere where it wouldn’t just be dismissed.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what you believe the coded messages or signals are specifically telling you to do, and do you feel that following them is leading you toward a unique or special outcome that most people wouldn't experience?\n\nPatient: I guess… specifically, it feels like they’re telling me which opportunities I’m supposed to move toward. Like if my classmate makes a certain comment in class, or the startup advisor posts something in Slack, I’ll read it as more than just casual—it feels like guidance, like I’m being nudged toward the career path I’m meant to take.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to say I’m destined to be famous or anything. It’s more like I’ve been really convinced they’re coordinating things quietly for me, and that if I follow the signals, I’ll end up in the right startup or role, something that’s kind of… specifically meant for me.\n\nI’m saying that okay, right? I know it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these signals or messages are happening consistently enough that you organize your decisions or actions around them, even if others might not see the same pattern?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I organize my whole life around it, but it has been consistent enough that I do notice it and kind of factor it in.\n\nLike, if that classmate says something in class, or the startup advisor posts something on Slack, I can get really focused on whether it’s a signal about what I’m supposed to do next career-wise. And I know other people probably wouldn’t read it that way, which is why I’ve been hesitant to say it too strongly.\n\nBut honestly, part of why I came in is that I’ve felt completely convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me. I try to keep it reasonable, but it does affect how I think about decisions. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that there’s a larger network or system involved—like people beyond your classmate and the advisor, or even technology or organizations—that’s part of coordinating these signals or your role?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole network or system, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more that I’ve been really convinced my MBA classmate and the startup advisor are, like, quietly coordinating opportunities for me. And then certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound dramatic or like some huge organization is involved. It’s just… with them specifically, it feels too connected to be random. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the signals or messages you’re receiving are meant only for you in a way that others couldn’t understand, or that you have a unique ability to recognize them that others don’t?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, kind of. I don’t mean that I’m special in some big way, or that nobody else could ever understand anything. I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and that certain things they say are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI’m trying to say it carefully because I don’t want it to sound… too much. Not like they’re trying to hurt me or trick me.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ve been convinced my MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me, and some comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded hints about where my career is supposed to go. I know that sounds like a lot when I say it out loud, so I’m trying not to overstate it.\n\nBut no, it doesn’t feel threatening. It feels like guidance, or like they’re trying to open doors for me. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you hear your name or phrases like 'go now,' is there usually something in your environment—like background noise, a TV, or music—that could explain the sound? I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s what happens. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do these times when you hear your name or phrases like 'go now' only happen when you're especially stressed, tired, or alone, or do they also happen when you're in a normal state or around other people?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m alone and working late, especially on school things or operations plans.
